- Security review (combined diff): Matrix and Mattermost tokens only in the `Authorization` header (socket upgrade included), `follow_redirects=False`, media only from the configured server with caps; Mattermost `@channel/@all/@here` defused, Matrix `m.mentions` with `@room` only when allowed; email From trusted only via the topmost `Authentication-Results` with the pinned authserv-id (DMARC / aligned SPF / aligned DKIM), header-only screening (64 KiB, From 2,048) before any body, HTML to text with nothing fetched; credentials scrubbed from errors/reprs; no subprocess, no argv. **Fixed:** Signal's event-stream URL carried the percent-encoded phone number, which httpx's INFO request line (and `response.request`) shows — the account is now added inside the transport (`_AccountQuery`); the masking test turns httpx logging on and fails without the fix (D-16.394). Noted, not changed: an operator-set http homeserver/Mattermost URL/signal-cli URL sends the token or number in clear (as Telegram's `base_url`); email TLS verification and plain IMAP/SMTP are opt-ins with warnings; relaxed DKIM/SPF alignment has no public-suffix list (needs an allowlisted address under a shared suffix to matter).
- **Bug found by the gate, fixed (P02-19, R03.8):** run 1 had one failure, `tests/integration/state/test_stress.py::test_store_survives_the_stress[host]` ("vacuum: 1 exceptions reached callers ['OperationalError']", the VACUUM worker failed after 1.03 s with "database is locked"); it passed 3/3 alone. Root cause: `maintenance.vacuum` ran `VACUUM` once on the borrowed writer connection, whose only patience was SQLite's 1 s busy timeout, so a sibling process holding the write lock a little longer (DELETE journaling under load) failed it — unlike every write, which waits within a time budget. New `Writer.patiently(conn, fn, budget=)` retries lock errors with the write path's pauses (then `StoreLocked`, which `indus sessions optimize` already reports); VACUUM uses it with the routine budget. Reproduced deterministically (a sibling takes `BEGIN IMMEDIATE` for 1.5 s right after the FTS merge): `test_maintenance.py::test_vacuum_waits_out_another_connections_write_lock` fails on main, passes with the fix; plus `test_writer.py::test_patiently_waits_out_a_lock_then_gives_up_at_the_deadline`; both on trap R03.8. Stress test 3/3 alone after the fix, and green in run 2.
- Intended differences recorded: D-16.380 … D-16.394, §2.7 (+ §3.1 row).
- Open seams / follow-ups: email lock key (address + IMAP host) needs a per-platform lock-key field; P16-32 target parsers (Matrix `split_target`, Signal `group.<id>`, email addresses); processing reactions (Matrix/Mattermost `processing_started/finished`) not called by the runner; R06.8 core order (adapter DM policy before the global `unauthorized_dm_behavior`); a `Capabilities` switch for "long text as an attachment" (email); `lacks` into `Capabilities`; `indus send` to Mattermost needs the `mattermost` extra because the registry probe is `httpx_ws` although sending needs only httpx; P16-07 must respect `send_only` when it starts E2EE; email allowlists must be written lower-case (the core compares exactly).
- Gate (verify7, 0a106d2 + E2 + E3 + integration, no extras installed, `uv sync --locked`): ruff check/format, pyright (0 errors), lint-imports (1 kept) clean; `INDUS_STRESS_SECONDS=10 pytest -m "not perf"` run 2: **12972 passed, 65 skipped, 11 deselected** (run 1: 12969 passed, 1 failed — the VACUUM bug above); `pytest -m perf` alone 11 passed; `tooling.checks --require-ref` ok; `CI=true pnpm test --maxWorkers=1` 855 passed (58 files); with `--extra mattermost` (separate venv) the Mattermost tests and contract case 37 passed, `test_the_real_opener_speaks_to_an_asgi_server` passed (skipped without the extra); no `# STAND-IN` file; no conflict markers; the staged diff has no credential-shaped literal; no stray processes.

## P16 E4 + E5 landing notes (integrator) — P16-04 WhatsApp Business Cloud, P16-10 SMS (E4); P16-23 LINE, P16-26 ntfy, P16-27 IRC (E5)
- Order applied on d50d5d3 (patch base 0a106d2), each `git apply --3way`: E4, then E5. Conflicts, all unions: config schema rows and `test_schema.py` (main's signal/matrix/mattermost/email + E4's `whatsapp_cloud`/`sms`), traps R06.3 (main's two Signal tests + E5's LINE wait test), contract `conftest.py` (one import per case, sorted: discord, email, irc, line, matrix, mattermost, ntfy, signal, slack, sms, telegram, whatsapp_cloud; E5's `lacks` comment naming `media`/`caption`), `test_adapter_contract.py` (E5's `media`/`caption` branches over main's inbound check). E4's copy of E3's `lacks`/`_long_text` hunks merged as no-ops. Traps R04.26, R06.4, R06.11, R06.24 merged clean; E5's IRC lock test, which its patch placed under R04.26, is now also on R06.16.
- Lead rulings applied: (1) **registry rows + `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS`** exactly as the README (`BUILTIN_NAMES` … email, whatsapp_cloud, sms, line, ntfy, irc, api_server, webhook); credentials for whatsapp_cloud, sms, line only. (2) **per-platform lock key** (D-16.412): `PlatformEntry.lock_env: tuple[str, ...]`; `gateway.config.lock_key(scope, settings, lock_env)` joins those values (read like credentials, in order) else the credential values; the daemon's `_lock_key` uses it, remembers the key it locked on (`_lock_keys`) and releases with that key (build failure, non-retryable connect error, fatal disconnect, stop). IRC = `IRC_SERVER` + `IRC_NICKNAME`, ntfy = `NTFY_TOPIC`, email = `EMAIL_ADDRESS` + `EMAIL_IMAP_HOST` (closes the E2+E3 seam: the password is no longer in the key), Signal unchanged (`SIGNAL_ACCOUNT` via its credentials). Lock files still `<platform>-<sha256[:16]>.lock`. Tests: `test_config.py::test_lock_keys_follow_the_registry_entry` (IRC order and channel-free; another nick → another key; email password change → same key, password not in it; Signal/Telegram unchanged), `irc/test_adapter.py::test_the_token_lock_key_is_server_and_nick` (two profiles on one server + nick collide, another nick acquires; nick/server not masked), and the daemon-level `test_daemon_start.py::test_the_lock_key_is_the_entrys_lock_env_when_it_names_one` (another profile holds server + nick → IRC `fatal token_conflict` while Telegram connects; nick `indus2` connects; fails without `lock_env`). (3) **IRC/ntfy names out of `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS`**: since that table also decides enablement, nick/server/topic would otherwise need `platforms.<name>.enabled: true`; new `BUILTIN_PUBLIC_ENV` (ntfy `NTFY_TOPIC`, irc `IRC_SERVER` + `IRC_NICKNAME`): all present turn the platform on from env/secret store, but the values are not in `PlatformSettings.credentials`, so nothing masks them (D-16.411; `test_public_names_turn_a_platform_on_but_are_not_credentials`). `test_plugin_platform_without_credentials_runs_on_config` used `irc` as its plugin example; it now uses `fakechat`.
- Wiring and moves: `mask_number` / `mask_numbers` moved from the WhatsApp Cloud and SMS adapters into `gateway/adapters/_identity.py` (re-exported by the adapters; new `test_identity.py::test_phone_numbers_are_masked_but_the_last_four_digits`); LINE/ntfy/IRC unit tests use the built-in tables (their private `credentials=` tables dropped); IRC and ntfy package docstrings name the lock seam.
- Acceptance: ACC-K9-2 (WhatsApp Cloud), ACC-K10-4 (SMS), ACC-K12-5 (LINE), ACC-K12-7 (ntfy), ACC-K12-8 (IRC) with contract + unit refs (K12 numbered by the platform's place in the inventory row: BlueBubbles 1 … Raft 11, so E6/E7 take Teams 3, Graph webhooks 4, Google Chat 6). Live lanes for Meta and Twilio left as Review lines (P16-36).
- Security review (combined diff): WhatsApp `X-Hub-Signature-256` and LINE `X-Line-Signature` checked on the raw body in constant time before any JSON (401); Twilio `X-Twilio-Signature` over the configured public URL (query kept, with/without default port) + sorted form fields in constant time (403) — the form is decoded first because the scheme signs the fields; body caps 3 MiB / 64 KiB / 1 MiB before reading; missing WhatsApp app secret and missing SMS public URL refuse the connect (fatal; send-only exempt); webhook listeners loopback by default; WhatsApp verify token compared in constant time; tokens, app secret, verify token, Twilio SID/token and both numbers scrubbed from errors/logs, reprs mask them (`TwilioSettings`, `CloudSettings`, `NtfyApi`, IRC passwords `repr=False`); ntfy `NTFY_TOKEN` refused at build for a non-https, non-loopback server, no redirects followed, Basic form scrubbed too; IRC refuses `PASS` / SASL / NickServ over a plain connection unless `allow_plaintext_auth` (`irc_plaintext_auth`, fatal), TLS with `create_default_context` + SNI; LINE served media `token_urlsafe(32)`, 1,800 s TTL (410), only media-policy paths, https public origin required. **Fixed:** a LINE channel token revoked while running only failed sends; a 401 now reports `line_auth` fatal (test `test_a_token_revoked_while_running_reports_the_fatal_state`, D-16.405). Noted, not changed: with httpx INFO logging turned on (the log setup pins it at WARNING) httpx's request line shows the Twilio Account SID (`/Accounts/<SID>/…`) and the Graph phone-number id (`/v23.0/<id>/…`) — identifiers, not secrets, but both are in `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS` and masked elsewhere; the Signal fix (inject inside the transport) would need the client factories to take a transport wrapper: follow-up. ntfy topics and IRC nicks/servers appear in logs by ruling (a topic on a public ntfy server is readable by anyone who knows it).
- Intended differences recorded: D-16.400 … D-16.414, §2.7 (+ §3.1 row).
- Open seams / follow-ups: lock keys for WhatsApp Cloud (token + phone-number id) and SMS (SID + token + number) still include the token — a rotated token gives a new key for the same number; `lock_env` = phone-number id / SID + number would match the email reasoning (lead call); ntfy lock key is the raw `NTFY_TOPIC` string (order-sensitive, overlapping topic lists not detected); email lock reads `EMAIL_IMAP_HOST` from env/secret store only (a host set only as `platforms.email.imap_host` gives an address-only key); httpx request-line identifiers above; WhatsApp `"whatsapp"` Markup style (P15-15); `lacks` into `Capabilities`; kit streaming replies (LINE 50 MiB serve cap); LINE `X-Line-Retry-Key`; interrupt seam for held LINE answers; ntfy inbound attachments; IRCv3 `account-tag`; WhatsApp voice transcoding, media by link, clarify "Other", health counters; SMS outbound MMS and an `INDUS_SMS_*` env bridge; `indus doctor` should list port 8646 (LINE), 8090 (WhatsApp), 8080 (SMS) (P16-36).
- Gate (verify8, d50d5d3 + E4 + E5 + integration, no extras installed, `uv sync --locked`): ruff check/format, pyright (0 errors), lint-imports (1 kept) clean; `INDUS_STRESS_SECONDS=10 pytest -m "not perf"` on the final tree: **13138 passed, 65 skipped, 11 deselected** (an earlier run, before the LINE fatal fix and doc edits, 13137 passed; one run on the final tree was cut off by a container restart at 8 % and repeated whole); `pytest -m perf` alone: first try 2 failed (`test_d04_1_criteria.py`, incl. `test_a_interrupt_mid_stream_closes_within_200ms[idle-stream]`) while another worktree's full suite was starting on the machine (load ≈ 2 on 4 cores; the wall-clock bounds are only meaningful alone, see "Gate change … perf tests run alone"; nothing in this diff touches the core turn/interrupt path), then 11 passed three times in a row; `tooling.checks --require-ref` ok; `CI=true pnpm test --maxWorkers=1` 855 passed (58 files); no `# STAND-IN` file; no conflict markers; the staged diff has no credential-shaped literal (Twilio `AC`/`SK` + 32 hex, Meta `EAA…`, Telegram, `xox*`, `ghp_`, `sk-`, `AKIA`, `glpat-`, bare 32-hex); no stray processes (three old `/tmp/indus-exec-*.pid` files from 2026-10-19, processes gone, predate this run).

## P17 wave 2 landing notes (integrator) — P17-08 daemon session host, P17-09 frozen creation policy (K3); P17-10 tickets + `ws_auth`, P17-11 attach verbs, P17-12 shared RPC dispatcher (K4)
- Order applied on c15f255 (base of both patches d50d5d3): K3, then K4, each `git apply --3way` (direct-application fallback); both clean, no conflict. Unions checked by hand: `foundation/errors.py` (K3's `runtime.policy_conflict` / `runtime.policy_refused` beside wave 1's `runtime.*`), traps R04.1 / R05.18 (K3) beside K4's rows, ACC-K32-2 / ACC-K51-3 (K4). No dependency change, no re-lock.
- **Stand-in removed** (`tests/unit/gateway/daemon/_stubs_k4.py` deleted; `grep -rln '^# STAND-IN' src tests` empty). `DaemonSessionHost` gained the two plain reads K4's `AttachHost` assumed: `creatable_sources()` = `cli tui gui acp web` (never `messaging`) and `capabilities()` = `events.since`, `server_requests`, `server_requests.<kind>` per kind, `request.decline` (the client feature vocabulary; P17-25 owns the table; D-16.433). `test_attach_verbs.py` runs on the real host: the round trip types it as `AttachHost`; "describe answers while a turn runs" parks a real host turn in a held tool, asks `describe` and `session-ticket` over the socket, checks the same generation still runs, releases and sees it complete.
- **Wiring done**: the daemon's control part (`side_services.DaemonBridge`) builds `AttachVerbs(home, TicketBook.for_home(home))` per daemon start, merges `attach_verbs.handlers()` into `control_handlers()` (`session-ticket` owner-only by name), and `ControlPart.stop` revokes the book after the socket stops (D-16.434; `test_control_part.py::test_the_daemon_serves_the_attach_verbs`). **Wiring owed** — P17-13: mount the WS endpoint and pass its `ws_url` (until then `session-ticket` answers `ws_unavailable`, `describe.ws_url` is null), `authenticate_upgrade` + `decode_request`/`Dispatcher`/`check_event` loop, `gateway.ready {replay_epoch, profile_id, instance_id, capabilities}` (the frame belongs to the WS endpoint: the protocol's `BridgeReady` model and the generated TS types gain the fields there, not hand-edited here). Building `DaemonSessionHost` inside the daemon is not possible yet: the daemon has one `SessionService` shared by the messaging pipeline and automation, and the host must own its service from the start (it replaces `broker_factory` and `turn_door`, which would move messaging onto the ledger) — that is P17-22 (messaging through `admit`, drain `gate=`), plus the owner epoch / `instance_id` source (the daemon's ownership lock and `boot.epoch`) and `aclose` ordering at stop (P17-13/P17-23). Then `AttachVerbs(host=daemon_host)`. P17-15/16/19: clients `describe` → `session_ticket` per dial. P17-18: alias resolution, refused-flag table, `--local` on the lease. P17-25: `policy_conflict` / `policy_refused` / `stale_generation` / ticket refusal reasons (`malformed unknown used expired wrong_purpose wrong_profile capacity`) and the deadline code onto the protocol table; `protocol_version` placement.
- **Lead ruling applied**: `indus-gateway-ticket.<t>` and a `ticket` query value are masked whole (`[redacted]`; before: first/last 4 kept) — `foundation/redact.py` full-mask patterns, any merged span touching one is masked whole; test `test_redact.py::test_ws_tickets_are_masked_whole` (fails on the patch as built) (D-16.429).
- **One vocabulary**: error codes `runtime.*` (incl. `policy_conflict`, `policy_refused`, `stale_generation`) in `ERROR_CODES`; ticket refusals are `TicketRefused.reason` and reach the control wire as the same strings (`capacity`); control-wire refusals are plain strings until P17-25; admission states stay K1's (`queued/started/terminal/unknown`), busy modes `queue`/`interrupt`. Layers: `ws_auth`, `rpc`, `daemon_host`, `viewers`, `creation_policy` in `service`; nothing in `service` imports `gateway`/`cli`/`tui_bridge`; `gateway.daemon.side_services` → `gateway.daemon.attach_verbs` → `service.ws_auth`; lint-imports kept.
- **Root cause of K3's five "load-only" failures** (`test_d04_1_terminal_backstop.py` ×2, `test_d04_1_terminal_tool_backstop.py` ×3): not load. `tests/unit/gateway/test_cron_host.py::test_cron_tick_refuses_while_a_live_gateway_owns_the_profile` runs `indus cron tick` in-process; its exit path (`cron_host.tick_main` → `terminal.runtime().shutdown()`) closes the shared environment registry and raises the process-wide one-way foreground fence, as a real exiting process must. Nothing lowered it, so every later foreground command in that pytest process answered rc 130 "[host is exiting: command not started]". The full suite never showed it because `tests/integration/core` sorts before `tests/unit/gateway`; K3's wider run listed the gateway paths first. Reproduced deterministically: that one cron test, then the two backstop files → 5 failed in 1 s; with a probe plugin it is the only test that leaves the fence up across `tests/unit/{service,gateway,cron,tui_bridge}`, `tests/contract`, `tests/integration/{gateway,cron}`. The product is right (the fence is for process exit; `indus cron tick` / `daemon`, `indus -z`, the gateway stop and the TUI bridge are process-level paths; nothing in a long-lived process calls `CronBackend.tick`); the flaw was test isolation, patched before per directory (`tests/unit/cli/conftest.py`, `test_plugin_host.py`, `test_saver.py`, `test_terminal_host.py`) but not for `tests/unit/gateway`. Fix: root `tests/conftest.py` autouse `reopen_after_a_host_exit` lowers the fence and drops a closed shared terminal runtime after every test (only when those modules are already imported). Regression test `tests/integration/core/test_d04_1_process_exit_isolation.py` (a host exit in the first test, a real `echo` in the second): the second fails without the fixture (rc 130), as do the backstop tests after the cron test. Not a wall-clock problem, so nothing moved to `perf`.
- **Security review.** (1) Tickets: 32 bytes from `secrets`, SHA-256 digest only, `hmac.compare_digest`, 30 s, single use, purpose-bound, instance-bound (book per start), `wrong_profile` when a profile is named, any failed redemption of a known ticket spends it, every ticket in a refused upgrade spent, query-string tickets spent and never used, logged without value; `MintedTicket`/`TicketReply` hide it from `repr`; the control socket logs the purpose only. **Fix:** spent tickets left digest markers that did not count toward the 1,024 cap, so an owner-side mint-and-burn loop grew the book without bound for 30 s windows; now at most 4 markers per live slot (`capacity`; `test_mint_and_burn_cannot_grow_the_book_without_bound`) (D-16.427). (2) `session-ticket` is owner-only by verb name inside `ControlServer` (forbidden when SO_PEERCRED cannot confirm the uid, even if registered without the flag; `test_owner_only_holds_even_when_registered_without_the_flag`); the socket file is 0600 and both sides refuse a group/world-writable home. (3) Loopback only: `authenticate_upgrade` refuses non-loopback and address-less peers (4403); the client refuses a `ws_url` that is not a credential-free loopback URL with a port. (4) RPC dispatcher: undeclared names refused at import/registration and answered `-32601`, unknown keys `UNKNOWN_PARAM_KEY` with the path, contract problems log the pydantic message only (no input), crashes log the method name only. Residual: a handler's own exception text reaches the traceback in the log (the log redactor masks credential shapes, not arbitrary values) — handlers must not put param values in exception messages. (5) Creation policy: `api_key` dropped; credential-shaped `key_ref` and `user[:pass]@` base URLs refused without echo. (6) **Fix:** viewers only see sessions they attached — fan-out was already per session key, but `events_since(..., connection_id=)` replayed any hosted session to any connection; now a non-viewer gets `SESSION_NOT_FOUND` (`test_a_connection_replays_only_sessions_it_attached`) (D-16.435). P17-13/14 still owe per-actor authorisation before every host call.
- Intended differences recorded: D-16.420 … D-16.435, §2.7 (+ §3.1 row).
- Follow-ups: `describe` advertises no capabilities until the daemon builds its host; the per-directory fence resets are now redundant with the root fixture (left in place); a dict key named `ticket` in a logged JSON object is not masked by shape (only the subprotocol and query forms are).
- Gate (verify9, c15f255 + K3 + K4 + integration, `uv sync --locked`, no extras): ruff check / format, pyright (0 errors, 0 warnings), lint-imports (1 kept) clean; `INDUS_STRESS_SECONDS=10 pytest -m "not perf"` **13122 passed, 65 skipped, 11 deselected** in one run (the E4+E5 integrator's full suite ran beside it; no failure); `pytest -m perf` alone 11 passed; `tooling.checks --require-ref` ok; `CI=true pnpm test --maxWorkers=1` 855 passed (58 files). No `# STAND-IN` file; no conflict markers; the diff has no credential-shaped literal (fake tickets in the new tests are generated at runtime); no stray process or socket from this worktree; nothing written under `/root` or `~` (a `~/indus-backup-2026-01-01-090000.zip` dated 2026-10-18 predates this run).

## P16 E6 + E7 + E8 landing notes (integrator) — P16-11 Microsoft Teams, P16-12 Graph change notifications (E6); P16-13 Google Chat, P16-20 QQ Bot (E7); P16-16 DingTalk, P16-17 WeCom smart robot, P16-18 WeCom callback (E8)
- Order applied on fa9d531 (patch base d50d5d3), each `git apply --3way`: E6, E7, then E8. Conflicts, all unions: config schema rows and `test_schema.py` (main's whatsapp_cloud/sms + teams/msgraph + google_chat/qqbot + dingtalk/wecom/wecom_callback), contract `conftest.py` (one import per case, sorted: dingtalk, discord, email, google_chat, irc, line, matrix, mattermost, ntfy, qqbot, signal, slack, sms, teams, telegram, wecom_callback, wecom, whatsapp_cloud; the `lacks` comment names `media`, `document`, `caption`, `voice_media`), `test_adapter_contract.py` (main's `media`/`caption` branches with E8's `voice_media` and `document` inside the media branch), traps R06.3 (main's LINE test + E7's QQ rate-limit test) and R06.24 (ntfy + Graph), `pyproject.toml` (extras `google-chat`, `qqbot`, `dingtalk`, `wecom`), `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (one cryptography/cffi/pycparser row naming the base install via mcp's `pyjwt[crypto]` for Teams and the `google-chat` / `wecom` extras; httpx-ws / wsproto rows name `dingtalk`, `wecom`, `qqbot`). 00-layout §4 pin rows (E8's httpx-ws and cryptography rows) extended to `qqbot` and `google-chat`.
- Lead rulings applied: (1) **Teams RS256** (D-16.441, D-16.451): the hand-rolled PKCS#1 v1.5 check (`pow(s, e, n)` + DigestInfo compare) is gone; new shared `gateway/adapters/_jws.py` (strict base64url, `rsa_public_key(n, e)`, `rs256_verify` over `cryptography`); `botauth.rsa_sha256_verify` keeps its interface (length check, then the library) and every claim check (RS256 allow-list, iss, aud, exp/nbf skew, kid cache + 5-minute refresh gap, 2,048–8,192-bit keys, endorsements, `serviceUrl`); Google Chat's verifier uses the same helper. Guard test `test_jws.py::test_no_rsa_arithmetic_of_our_own_in_src` scans `src/indus` for a three-argument `pow(` or a SHA-256 DigestInfo prefix (it flags the builder's file). (2) **Graph replies** (D-16.443): with no route a reply is `msgraph.adapter.not_delivered()` — `ok` false, `unsupported` (the core neither retries nor sends a notice), `extra["undelivered"] == "not_configured"`, counted, one warning per receiver naming `platforms.msgraph.deliver_to` (or the missing wiring); never reported delivered. (3) **PEM literal**: Google Chat's test builds the armour at run time and also uses a real throwaway EC key for the non-RSA case. (4) **Registry rows + credentials** as the README (D-16.453); hints written at landing; `msgraph` in `send_command._NOT_TARGETS` and the channel directory's `_NOT_TARGETS`; `wecom_callback` added to `cron.targets.KNOWN_PLATFORMS` (msgraph deliberately not). (5) **Lock keys** (D-16.454): teams client id + tenant id, qqbot app id, dingtalk client id, wecom bot id, wecom_callback corp id + agent id; google_chat (key JSON) and msgraph (clientState) keep the credential. A `BUILTIN_PUBLIC_ENV` row for the agent id was tried and dropped: the table must stay disjoint from `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS` (existing invariant test); the agent id is read by the adapter (env/secret or `platforms.wecom_callback.agent_id`) and by the lock key from env/secret only.
- Wiring: **drain probe** as a base seam (D-16.452): `Adapter.set_drain_probe` / `draining()`; the daemon installs `lifecycle.notice` on every platform up and removes it in `disconnect_platform`; Google Chat's own copy removed. **Graph forward**: `RouterPart` gives every `GraphReceiver` that comes up `delivery.parts.graph_forward(router)` (one explicit `platform:chat` on another platform through `DeliveryRouter.send`; anything else `bad_format`, a failed delivery final) and never attaches the receiver as a target; `deliver_to: msgraph:…` is a config error. **`indus send`**: `tests/unit/gateway/delivery/test_send_p16_platforms.py` builds teams, google_chat, qqbot, dingtalk and wecom through the real `send_command.build_adapter` with the built-in registry rows and the enablement pass (only the package's `create_adapter` is wrapped to pass the wire fake's transport/opener); qqbot/dingtalk/wecom force only the row's `httpx_ws` probe, which a send does not need (same seam as Mattermost's, follow-up). Still owed: WeCom native streams need the runner seam (P16-33, `streaming_default("wecom")`); WeCom inbound media has no private-address guard — neither the kit nor `_identity` has one; `indus.safety.safe_transport` is the seam (follow-up, recorded in `wecom.md`).
- **Bug found by the gate, fixed (E7 test fake):** `qqbot/test_upload.py::test_parts_run_in_parallel_only_as_far_as_allowed` failed once in the first adapter run (`max_parallel_puts == 1`) and passed 3/3 alone. Root cause: the fake's part PUT held for one loop iteration only, so whether two PUTs overlapped depended on when each part's worker-thread file read finished. Reproduced deterministically by staggering the reads (`_read_part` sleeping 20 ms × offset: fails); the fake now has a count-based `put_gate` (PUTs wait until that many run at once, an `asyncio.Event`, no timing) and the test asserts exactly 3; the staggered repro passes.
- Acceptance: ACC-K11-1 (DingTalk), ACC-K11-3 (WeCom and WeCom callback), ACC-K11-5 (QQ Bot), ACC-K12-3 (Teams), ACC-K12-4 (Graph webhooks), ACC-K12-6 (Google Chat), numbered by place in the inventory rows, with contract + unit refs and live lanes as Review lines (P16-36); the Teams meeting pipeline stays a K12 gap (D-16.87).
- Security review (combined diff): Teams — token checked before the body; RS256 only, `none`/HMAC/other algs refused before key lookup, `crit` refused, header URLs never fetched, keys only from the metadata host over https, kid refresh rate-limited, key size bounds, iss/aud/exp/nbf; service URLs exact https hosts on 443 without userinfo/query, refused ones never stored or called; the bot token only to Bot Connector hosts. Graph — validation echo ≤1,024 printable characters, `text/plain` with the kit's `nosniff`; clientState 16–128, constant time, first; notifications internal, `allow_control` false, sanitised fields only; public bind needs source CIDRs. Google Chat — RS256 pinned, JWK sets only from https googleapis hosts, 300 s cache + one forced refetch a minute; key JSON inline only, Google-only `token_uri`; exact media host list. QQ — token in the `Authorization` header only, never logged (the app id appears in one INFO line, as an identifier); media only from https QQ hosts with the token. DingTalk / WeCom callback — secrets added inside the transport (not in httpx's request line or `response.request`); constant-time signatures, ±300 s replay window, hardened expat. Credentials masked in reprs/errors (adapters' scrub over `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS` + extra secrets). httpx request lines: Teams conversation ids, Google space names and QQ openids appear in URLs (identifiers, not credentials; not made worse than the E4+E5 follow-up). Noted, not changed: a Teams service-URL path is checked for `..`/`//` but not percent-encoded dots (the host stays a Microsoft host); the WeCom media guard above.
- Intended differences recorded: D-16.440 … D-16.455, §2.7 (+ §3.1 row).
- Open seams / follow-ups: send-only probe (qqbot/dingtalk/wecom/mattermost rows probe `httpx_ws`, which a send does not use); Google Chat lock key on the key JSON (a `lock_env` cannot reach the client email inside it); `whatsapp_cloud` missing from `cron.targets.KNOWN_PLATFORMS` (E4; works only while live); WeCom media private-address guard; P16-32 target parsers (Teams, QQ prefixes, DingTalk `user:`); Teams `allowed_tenants`, proactive DM to new users, file consent; Google Chat native uploads (per-user OAuth); QQ webhook mode; WeCom template cards and native-stream runner seam (P16-33); doctor port table 3978 / 8645 (WeCom callback, clashes with BlueBubbles) / 8646 (Graph and LINE share it) / 8647 (P16-36); ACC live lanes.

## P16 E9 + E10 landing notes (integrator) — P16-14 Feishu / Lark, P16-15 Feishu document tools and comment intake (E9); P16-24 BlueBubbles, P16-28 SimpleX, P16-29 Buzz (E10)
- Order applied on 9093b78 (patch base d50d5d3), each `git apply --3way`: E9, then E10. Conflicts, all unions: config schema rows and `test_schema.py` (main's whatsapp_cloud … wecom_callback + feishu + bluebubbles/simplex/buzz), contract `conftest.py` (one import per case, sorted: bluebubbles, buzz, dingtalk, discord, email, feishu, google_chat, irc, line, matrix, mattermost, ntfy, qqbot, signal, simplex, slack, sms, teams, telegram, wecom_callback, wecom, whatsapp_cloud), `pyproject.toml` (extras `feishu`, `simplex`, `buzz` after main's `google-chat`, `qqbot`, `dingtalk`, `wecom`), `THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES.md` (httpx-ws / wsproto rows name `feishu`, `simplex`, `buzz` too; E10's coincurve row, which now also names the bundled `_cffi_backend`), 00-layout §4 (one httpx-ws row extended to `feishu`, `simplex`, `buzz`; new coincurve row), acceptance map (E10's ACC-K12-1 placed before K12-3, K12-9/10 after K12-8). Traps R02.25 merged clean.
- Lead rulings applied: (1) **registry rows + `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS`** as the README (D-16.473): `BUILTIN_NAMES` … wecom_callback, feishu, bluebubbles, simplex, buzz, api_server, webhook, msgraph (the README's "after email" predates the E4–E8 rows, so they follow wecom_callback); hints for BlueBubbles, SimpleX and Buzz written at landing from the adapters' markup; credentials feishu app id + secret, bluebubbles password, buzz private key. **SimpleX deviates from the README**: `SIMPLEX_WS_URL` is in `BUILTIN_PUBLIC_ENV`, not `BUILTIN_CREDENTIALS` (D-16.474) — it is the only enabling value and not a secret (the daemon has none), so it turns SimpleX on and keys the lock like ntfy's topic and IRC's server/nick but is never masked; the package exports `PUBLIC_ENV` instead of `CREDENTIALS`. (2) **lock keys** (D-16.475): feishu `FEISHU_APP_ID`; bluebubbles `BLUEBUBBLES_SERVER_URL`; simplex `SIMPLEX_WS_URL`; Buzz keeps the credential (the private key): it is one-to-one with the npub, the derived pubkey is not readable from env, and the relay URL alone would make two bots on one relay collide. **Core change** (D-16.476): `gateway.config.lock_key` falls back to the credential values when none of the entry's `lock_env` values is in env/secret store, instead of returning "" (no lock) — BlueBubbles' URL may live only in `platforms.bluebubbles.server_url`; no existing entry changes (IRC/ntfy have no credentials; the E4–E8 `lock_env` names include a credential). Test `test_config.py::test_p16_e9_e10_lock_keys` (also on trap R06.16). (3) **P16-15 core wiring** (D-16.466): `tools/toolsets.py` gains `"feishu"` (`platforms=frozenset({"feishu"})`), `"indus-feishu"` (`includes=(core, "feishu")`) and `indus-feishu` in `indus-gateway`'s includes; new `gateway.daemon.process.gateway_tool_registry(scope)` (`load_builtins` + `feishu.tools.register_tools`) builds every daemon session service's registry; `"feishu_"` added to `core/untrusted.UNTRUSTED_PREFIXES` (the tools fence their own output too; the module rule is "no already-fenced shortcut, fencing twice is harmless"). The builder's monkeypatched rows are gone; new tests `test_tools.py::test_the_feishu_rows_are_in_the_toolset_table` (`default_toolsets("feishu") == ["indus-feishu"]`, untrusted prefix) and `::test_only_the_gateway_daemon_registers_the_tools` (in the daemon registry, absent from a plain `load_builtins` registry; with `indus-gateway` selected only Feishu sessions get them; every-toolset sessions elsewhere do not), both on R02.25 (its Review line now names only the Yuanbao tools).
- **Re-lock**: `uv lock` added one package, coincurve 21.0.0 (no dependencies), plus extras metadata (`feishu`, `simplex`, `buzz`: `provides-extras`, `requires-dist`, optional-deps). Licence read from the installed dist-info in a throwaway venv (scratchpad, offline from the uv cache): `License-Expression: MIT OR Apache-2.0`, licence files LICENSE-MIT, LICENSE-APACHE, LICENSE-cffi, NOTICE; the NOTICE says the wheels bundle cffi's `_cffi_backend` (MIT) and libsecp256k1 (MIT) — nothing outside the allow-list (D-16.472). 00-layout §4 pin row added; `tooling.checks pins/licenses` ok.
- **`indus send`** (D-16.477): `delivery/test_send_p16_platforms.py` gains feishu, bluebubbles, simplex and buzz through the real `send_command.build_adapter` with the built-in rows and the enablement pass (the package's `create_adapter` wrapped only to pass the wire fake's transport/opener/signer): Feishu opens no socket and calls neither the endpoint nor `bot/v3/info`; BlueBubbles registers no webhook and does not listen; SimpleX and Buzz force only the row's probe — their sockets (and Buzz's signer) are their only transports, so unlike Mattermost/QQ/DingTalk/WeCom the probe is right and a real `indus send` needs the extra.
- Ports (D-16.479, for P16-36, nothing renumbered — the phase file names 8645 for BlueBubbles): BlueBubbles 8645 = WeCom callback default; Graph and LINE both 8646; Feishu webhook 8765; Teams 3978; Google Chat 8647. A clash fails the second platform's connect with a retryable `listener_bind` (it keeps retrying); `webhook_port` under the platform block moves one.
- Security review (combined diff): Feishu — the signature (SHA-256 of timestamp + nonce + encrypt key + raw body) is compared in constant time before any parse, ±300 s; encrypted mode refuses a plaintext body (400) and decrypts with `cryptography` AES-256-CBC (PKCS#7 checked; every decrypt/JSON failure the same 400, so no padding oracle); the verification token in constant time, the URL challenge echoed only after it, other unsigned events 401; webhook mode needs the token; event-id dedupe; JSON only; loopback listener; the long-connection ticket URL added inside the transport and masked; app secret only in bodies. Doc tools — fenced (own fence + the tool round's), 30,000-character windows, 2 MiB document cut, 40,000 registered cap, links parsed for a token only on https Feishu/Lark hosts and never opened, write tools text-only and escaped. BlueBubbles — `?token=` is an HMAC of the password compared in constant time before parsing; the password only inside the transport (`_PasswordQuery`), masked with the token; access logs off in the kit. SimpleX — loopback only unless `allow_remote`; `/_send` targets digits only (no command injection), `/freceive` ids digits only; inbound files read only after `resolve(strict=True)` inside `files_folder`, ≤100 MiB; `auto_accept` off. Buzz — every inbound event's NIP-01 id recomputed and its BIP-340 signature verified by coincurve before use; secret only in `NostrKeys` (`repr=False`) and the adapter's scrub list; NIP-42 auth signed. No subprocess, no argv secrets in any of the five. **Fixed:** Buzz remembered every dropped (forged/malformed) event id in an unbounded set (log-once dedupe), so a relay streaming junk grew memory without bound — capped at `BAD_CAP` 2,048 (`test_dropped_event_ids_are_remembered_only_up_to_a_cap`). **Guard added:** `buzz/test_crypto.py::test_no_curve_arithmetic_of_our_own_in_src` (secp256k1 field prime, order and generator fragments anywhere under `src/indus`, the `_jws` guard's style). **Literal fixed:** the NIP-19 example key's hex form in `buzz/test_crypto.py` was a bare 64-hex literal; now built by concatenation like its nsec form. Noted, not changed: the Feishu doc read cut is applied after the vendor's (authenticated, fixed-host) answer is in memory — no streaming byte cap on API answers (follow-up); Buzz's NIP-01 serialisation uses `json.dumps`, which escapes control characters other than `\b\t\n\f\r` as `\u00XX` where NIP-01 keeps them verbatim — such (rare) events fail verification and are dropped, never accepted (interop follow-up, not a bypass); the BIP-340 fixture holds the spec's four public test secret keys (vector data, `bitcoin/bips`).
- Acceptance: ACC-K11-2 (Feishu / Lark, adapter + tools + `indus send`), ACC-K12-1 / 9 / 10 gain their `indus send` refs; live lanes Review lines (P16-36 / owner).
- Intended differences recorded: D-16.460 … D-16.479, §2.7 (+ §3.1 row).
- Open seams / follow-ups: Feishu streaming byte cap for API answers; Buzz control-character serialisation (NIP-01 exact escaping), cursor persistence, edits/reactions/files/NIP-OA, the 16,000 cap and DM detection need a live check; Feishu live check of token refusal codes and long-connection fields, processing reactions not called by the runner, voice transcoding, continuation delay, per-IP webhook rate limit; SimpleX thumbnails; BlueBubbles' kit listener is loopback by default, so a server on another Mac needs `webhook_host` + source CIDRs (doc for P16-31 setup); P16-31 setup wizards, P16-32 target parsers, P16-36 doctor ports above; `whatsapp_cloud` still missing from `cron.targets.KNOWN_PLATFORMS` (E4; feishu/bluebubbles/simplex/buzz were already there).
- Gate (verify11, 9093b78 + E9 + E10 + integration, no extras installed, `uv sync --locked`): ruff check/format, pyright (0 errors), lint-imports (1 kept) clean; `INDUS_STRESS_SECONDS=10 pytest -m "not perf"`: **13842 passed, 76 skipped, 11 deselected** (first run, no failures); `pytest -m perf` alone 11 passed; `tooling.checks --require-ref` ok; `CI=true pnpm test --maxWorkers=1` 855 passed (58 files); with `--extra buzz --extra feishu --extra simplex` (separate scratch venv, `uv sync --locked --offline`) the four adapters' tests, the contract suite and the send tests 531 passed / 38 skipped (only discord/slack vendor-name rows), including `buzz/test_crypto.py::test_bip340_vectors_verify_as_the_spec_says`, `::test_bip340_signatures_round_trip_and_events_verify` and Feishu's `test_the_real_opener_speaks_to_an_asgi_server` (skipped without the extras); no `# STAND-IN` file; no conflict markers; the staged diff has no credential-shaped literal (PEM armour, `eyJ…eyJ`, `nsec1` + key, `ghp_`, `sk-`, `xox*`, `AKIA`, `glpat-`, Meta `EAA…`, Twilio `AC`/`SK` + 32 hex, `AIza`; bare 64-hex only in uv.lock hashes, the BIP-340 vector fixture and Feishu signature vectors); no stray processes.

  KNOWN_PLATFORMS.

## P17-15..22 landing notes (integrator) — P17-22 messaging on the shared host (K9); P17-15 ensure, P17-16 attach channel, P17-17 remote session host (K6); P17-18 CLI attach, P17-21 store ownership (K7); P17-19 TUI attach, P17-20 `@indus/client` WS transport (K8)
- Order applied on f6250bd (base of all four patches ac389c3): K9, K6, K7, K8, each `git apply --3way` (direct-application fallback). Conflicts, all unions: traps R09.11 (K6's host tests beside K7's CLI tests, K7's ACP review line kept) and R05.9 (K6's channel tests beside K8's TS test; the stale "not yet planned" line dropped); ACC-K51-1/K51-2 (K7's and K8's rows) and ACC-K51-4 (K6's and K8's tests); `cli/commands/gateway.py` lazy rows (`setup` from main beside K8's `grant`, K6's `ensure` already merged). `side_services.py` merged clean (K9's `AttachVerbs(host=_HostView, ws_url=daemon.ws_url)` over `daemon.tickets` beside K7's `MaintenanceVerb` registration), `control_socket.py` (`maintenance-run` known and owner-only), `protocol/methods.py` + regenerated TS (K6's `PromptSubmitParams.request_id`), `gateway/ws/methods.py`, non_tty_table (`gateway ensure`, `gateway grant` long-running). No dependency change, no re-lock.
- **Stand-ins removed** (`grep -rln '^# STAND-IN' src tests` empty). K7: `attach.backend_factory` → new `cli/attach_remote.DaemonBackend` (ensure = `service.gateway_ensure.ensure(home, env, mode="start")`, not-attachable verdict or null `ws_url` → `AttachUnavailable` exit 1; connect = `Channel(home_dialer(home, env=, budget=RecoverBudget()), surface="cli")` + `RemoteSessionService(channel, Journal(home)).start()` wrapped in `RemoteAttached`); name-loading (`ENSURE_ENTRY`/`REMOTE_ENTRY`) dropped (the CLI may import `indus.service`); `_stubs_k7.py` became `tests/unit/cli/attach_double.py`, a double of the CLI's own seams. K7's assumed extras mapped: `create(policy=)` sends `cwd`/`model`/`toolsets` (what `session.create` carries) and every other given frozen field is refused before ensure, exit 2 naming `--local` (D-16.490); `resume(policy=)` → id only (the read-only frozen check runs first); `submit(unattended=)` dropped; `wait` → `AttachedTurn` with the reply streamed after the last tool call (the daemon emits no `message.complete`); `OutcomeUnknown` → `outcome_unknown`, or `blocked_by_unknown` when our admission is still queued behind an unknown one; `pending` = read-only runtime-ledger read (D-16.495); `discard_unknown` = new daemon-only `admission.discard` (D-16.496); Ctrl-C interrupts only our own turn's generation (`RemoteSessionService.turn_of`, D-16.499). Added to K6's host (tested): `key_of`, `turn_of`, `discard_unknown`. K8: `_stubs_k8.py` deleted; `gateway_grant.ensure_endpoint(scope, *, mode, timeout)` runs K6's ensure in the grant's mode and keeps `Ensured.terminal` (`planned_stop` terminal with a hint); tickets via `remote.channel.grant_for`; ids changed between ensure and mint → `stale_instance` retry; describe-only fallback and `no_ensure` removed (D-16.493).
- **Lead decision applied**: `AdmissionService.discard_unknown(id, generation, *, terminal_write=None)` → `ledger.resolve_unknown(terminal_write=)`; `DaemonSessionHost.discard_unknown` passes it on; `admissions.close_lost_turn` appends a short assistant note when the lost turn's session ends with the person's message (skips no row / no messages / a reply already there / a compressed-away session). Wired into `recovery.resolve_unknown` and the WS `admission.discard`; not into the recovered-reply discard (transcript already ends with the stored final). Rippled no further than admissions/recovery/host/WS (D-16.497).
- **End to end** (`tests/integration/cli/test_attach_daemon.py`, real `indus gateway run` in a temp home with temp `HOME`/config, loopback fake model): `indus chat -q` attached (no `--local`) prints the daemon's answer; a second client (the CLI's own `DaemonBackend` → `AttachedHost.feed`) resumes that session and sees another CLI process's turn live (`turn.started`, deltas, `turn.done`); `-z` attached; `indus gateway grant --json` → `@indus/client` (`tests/contract/client/ts_grant_script.ts`, grant on stdin) attaches through it and runs a turn, ticket absent from its output, logs and the daemon's logs; `sessions delete` runs inside the daemon (`maintenance delete runs in the daemon`), `indus import` refused; every turn admitted once (`attach:cli` / `tui:<conn>`); cold start: `chat -q` with no daemon starts one detached (not our child) and attaches; `--local` answers in-process and starts nothing (no ledger row). Plus `tests/unit/cli/test_attach_remote.py` (real backend over the real WS endpoint on loopback: chat/-z, exit 3 behind a lost turn, `/discard` closes it and the queue goes on).
- **Security review.** (1) Tickets: only inside `protocols` on the grant's stdout (TS script gets them on stdin), never in URL/argv/log (unit + e2e checks; K6 channel/repr checks). (2) Loopback-only dials: K6 `ws_client` (`ws://` loopback, no proxy), `ws_url_is_safe` on the ticket client, the TUI validates the grant URL. (3) Owner-only verbs: `session-ticket` and `maintenance-run` both in `OWNER_ONLY_VERBS`; new test registers `maintenance-run` without the flag (as the daemon does) and checks an unverified peer is `forbidden`. (4) Journal entries 0600 in a 0700 dir (K6 test). (5) **Fix:** `ensure` scrubbed only the five switch vars from a daemon it starts, so `INDUS_MODEL`/`INDUS_TUI_*` from a TUI launch reached the shared daemon; `creation_policy.scrub_launch_env` now drops the switch vars, `INDUS_MODEL`, `INDUS_PROVIDER`, `INDUS_CWD`, `INDUS_LAUNCH_CWD` and every `INDUS_TUI_*`, and the grant helper uses the same function (D-16.498; tests in `test_creation_policy.py`, `test_ensure.py`). (6) Outcome-unknown never resent: CLI (journal kept as unknown, no retry; submit-time loss now handled like a wait-time loss), TUI (startup prompt sent once, recovery only reopens; by inspection), gateway recovery (K9 tests). (7) **Fix:** a Ctrl-C on a finite run whose input was queued behind another surface's turn interrupted that other turn (the submit answers the running generation); now only our own turn is interrupted. (8) `admission.discard` checks the admission belongs to the connection's session (else `not_found`). Residual: `gateway_control.query` does not check the listener's uid (K6 follow-up; the socket lives in the 0700 home).
- Intended differences recorded: D-16.480 … D-16.499, §2.7 (+ §3.1 row).
- **Remaining for P17-23..27.** P17-23: read `INDUS_GATEWAY_AUTOSTART`; idle exit must ignore `messaging` viewers (the intake views every chat session it touched). P17-24: profile guard module (grant/channel already refuse another `profile_id`); client-side listener uid check. P17-25: creation policy on `session.create`/`resume` (lifts D-16.490's refusal; the daemon must also *apply* the frozen provider/switches, today only recorded), `deliver` on submits (D-16.485), `outcome_unknown`/`stale_generation`/`policy_conflict` error codes, `admission.state`/`admission.receipt`, `message.complete`/`session.usage` from the daemon (finite reply, usage file api_calls), `runtime.describe`, `maintenance.run` over WS, `session.mutate`/`redirect` (attached slash subset), compaction child ids. P17-26: dual-host matrix (the REPL over `RemoteAttached` is only unit-tested with the double); TUI `session.steer` generation; attach-mode "restarting" wording. P17-27: shared-backend demo (CLI + TUI on one session, approval across surfaces, daemon restart → both report unknown). Also: `--local` onto the durable lease (D-16.488); REPL end on channel loss; resume recap needs a daemon snapshot; backups still include ledger tables (R03.21).
- Gate (verify14, f6250bd + K9 + K6 + K7 + K8 + integration, `uv sync --locked`, no extras): ruff check / format, pyright (0 errors, 0 warnings), lint-imports (1 kept) clean; `INDUS_STRESS_SECONDS=10 pytest -m "not perf"` **14223 passed, 76 skipped, 11 deselected, 1 failed**: `tests/unit/backends/ssh/test_environment.py::test_home_detection_retried_no_sooner_than_30s` (not P17 code) passes alone 3×; root cause: the test's `drive` helper advances the fake clock 0.05 s per ~2 ms of real waiting while `connect` keeps running after the failed home lookup, so under load more than 5 s of fake time pass after `_home_failed_at` and the later `advance(HOME_RETRY_S - 5)` already reaches 30 s — a test-timing flaw (follow-up: measure from the failure, or stop driving after it), nothing marked or skipped. The first full run had one P17 failure, fixed: a cold `ensure` returned before the WebSocket was mounted (see D-16.481; the e2e cold-start test then passed 3× alone and once under 4-core CPU load). `pytest -m perf` alone 11 passed; `tooling.checks --require-ref` ok; `CI=true pnpm test --maxWorkers=1` 899 passed; `pnpm typecheck` (protocol, ui, client, tui) clean; biome clean on apps/packages/tests/contract/client (repo-wide `pnpm lint` still flags pre-existing `tests/fixtures/**/*.json` formatting, untouched here). No `# STAND-IN` file, no conflict markers, no credential-shaped literal in the diff (fake tokens built at runtime); every daemon the tests started was stopped (no `gateway run` or node process left); nothing written under `/root/.indus` (last write 08:17, the K8 incident).
- Lead fix at landing (test only): `tests/unit/backends/ssh/test_environment.py::test_home_detection_retried_no_sooner_than_30s`
  failed once in the integrator's full run. Cause: `drive` advances the fake clock 0.05 s per ~2 ms of real waiting while the
  fake ssh runs, so fake time passed inside connect() and the fixed `advance(HOME_RETRY_S - 5)` could already reach the
  30 s retry point on a loaded machine. The test now measures from the recorded failure time and drives the "not yet due"
  command with a 0.001 s step; product behaviour (retry spaced 30 s from the failure) unchanged. 3/3 alone and 3/3 under
  pinned CPU load.

- **Lead decision, Baileys (owner has not answered)**: shipped without the library mapping. `bindBaileys`, the Baileys message reader/error mapper and the folder lookup of the library package were deleted from `apps/whatsapp-bridge/src/provider.ts`; `provider.test.ts` rewritten (provider loading, `WaError`); the fake-provider tests (session, HTTP, main, the real bridge from Python) stay. Note: provider is operator-supplied through the WaSocket interface; no provider ships; Baileys pending an owner decision (copyleft deps). The bridge still starts and fails closed: without `INDUS_WA_PROVIDER` it prints `{"event":"error","error":"no_provider"}` and exits 2 before binding (`main.test.ts`), and the adapter turns that exit into the non-retryable `whatsapp_provider_missing` with a message naming `platforms.whatsapp.provider` (`test_sidecar.py`, and `test_real_bridge.py` against the real Node bridge). The P16 phase file's interop row, the P16-02 row and its session.ts file row now name the operator-supplied provider (D-16.531).
- **Extras and the lock — stopped at the re-lock**: extras `matrix-e2ee` (Linux-only markers; mautrix 0.21.1, python-olm 3.2.16, pycryptodome 3.23.0, unpaddedbase64 2.1.0, base58 2.1.1, aiosqlite 0.22.1, asyncpg 0.31.0), `weixin` (cryptography 50.0.2) and `yuanbao` (httpx-ws 0.9.0) are in `pyproject.toml`, but `uv lock --offline` cannot resolve them (asyncpg 0.31.0 and pycryptodome 3.23.0 metadata for the Python 3.14 split are not in the uv cache) and the brief allows no download, so **`uv.lock` was not regenerated and not hand-edited**. Consequence: `uv sync --locked` refuses until the lead runs `uv lock` online; the gate below ran on `uv sync --frozen` (base dependencies unchanged, extras not installed). Licences read from the installed dist-info of the builder's scratch venv: mautrix `Classifier: MPL 2.0` (file-level copyleft, allow-listed for unmodified dependency use, never patched or vendored), python-olm `Apache-2.0` (bundles libolm, Apache-2.0), pycryptodome `BSD, Public Domain` (LICENSE.rst: BSD-2-Clause + public domain), unpaddedbase64 `Apache-2.0`, base58 `MIT`, aiosqlite MIT classifier, asyncpg `License-Expression: Apache-2.0`; no other copyleft, no `licenses_allow.json` change. THIRD_PARTY_NOTICES gains a libolm row (deprecated upstream, timing side-channel advisories, behind our Engine interface) and names weixin/matrix-e2ee on pycparser; 00-layout §4 gains the seven matrix-e2ee pin rows (upload dates read offline from the uv index cache: mautrix 2026-07-05, python-olm 2023-11-28, pycryptodome 2025-05-17, unpaddedbase64 2021-03-09, base58 2021-10-30, aiosqlite 2025-12-23, asyncpg 2025-11-24) and the weixin use on the cryptography row.
- **Security review (combined diff)**. Matrix: room keys only to devices at or above the trust floor (owner cross-signed by default, `e2ee_trust: verified` stricter) of allowed users, blocked devices never; store 0700, database and lock 0600, `flock` per device store (second process → retryable `e2ee_store_busy`); `MATRIX_RECOVERY_KEY` only from the SecretStore (never env/config) and scrubbed; `indus send` into an encrypted room refused, and any encryption-state answer other than `M_NOT_FOUND` fails the send (never clear text). Weixin: context tokens and cursor written atomically 0600 in a 0700 directory; inbound media URLs https on Tencent hosts only; the iLink base must be https. **Fix:** the configured CDN base (`cdn_base_url` / `WEIXIN_CDN_BASE_URL`, used for every media upload and download) was not checked — it now must be https on a Tencent media host, else connect fails with a non-retryable `config` error (new test `test_a_cdn_base_outside_tencents_https_hosts_is_refused_at_connect`). WhatsApp: bridge binds loopback only (non-loopback exits 2), `Host` must be loopback, Bearer token per start compared in constant time (SHA-256 then `timingSafeEqual`), 64 KiB body cap, auth folder 0700 / umask 077, token only in the child's env and a 0600 file (never argv; checked against `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` in the real-bridge test), child env from the minimal builder; the adapter adopts only its own healthy bridge and never signals another process. Yuanbao: app secret and sign tokens masked in logs, errors and reprs; `yuanbao_send_dm` text-only, current-group member, the gateway's Authorizer, and the approval gate on every call (pattern `yuanbao:dm:<recipient>`, so a "for the session" answer covers later DMs to that member only). Raft: `bridge.json` 0600 in a 0700 directory, token compared in constant time, loopback peers only. Vendor tokens in argv or logs: none found.
- **Fix (test, root-caused):** `weixin/test_adapter.py::test_the_cursor_is_saved_after_dispatch_and_resumed_after_a_restart` failed once in a large targeted run and passed alone 3×. Cause: the adapter writes the context token and the cursor on the io pool with an fsync before (token) and after (cursor) dispatch, and the test waited a fixed 6 × 5 ms of real time; a busy disk holds an fsync longer. Reproduced deterministically by slowing `os.fsync` by 80 ms (fails at the events assertion); the test now waits in real time, fake clock still, until the event and `cursor.json` exist, and until the restarted adapter's first poll — passes with the slowed fsync and 3× alone; product unchanged.
- Intended differences recorded: D-16.524 … D-16.539, §2.7 (+ §3.1 row).
- **Follow-ups**: lead re-lock online (`uv lock`, then `uv sync --locked` and the extras lane: matrix with python-olm from the sdist, yuanbao's real opener with httpx-ws); vodozemac engine when mautrix supports it; `indus send` into encrypted Matrix rooms through the daemon; Weixin QR login and WhatsApp pairing (`INDUS_WA_MODE=pair`) in `indus gateway setup` (P16-31); packaging the bridge for wheel installs (P23/P25); canonical WhatsApp participant alias folding in `session_keys.canonical_participant`; bridge proxy support; `.nvmrc` Node 24 vs the sandbox's Node 22; Yuanbao tools' `current_identity()` under P17-22 session-host tasks (session-id → route lookup); a smart-mode judge can still approve `yuanbao_send_dm` like any gated tool (consider person-only for messages to people); Yuanbao/Weixin/Raft/Matrix-E2EE live lanes (iLink names, `auth-bind` instance id, Yuanbao kick behaviour of the send-only socket) for P16-36 / owner; Raft `/health` activity summary and a supervised `raft agent bridge` child; doctor ports 3000/3001–3999 (WhatsApp bridge) for P16-36; HANDOFF still names the Baileys dependency chain in its owner-decision list (lead's file); the E11 rows D-16.520 … D-16.523 sit under §3.1 instead of §2.7.

## P17-23..27 landing notes (integrator) — P17-23 idle exit (L1); P17-24 profile binding, P17-25 protocol additions and codegen (L2); P17-26 dual-host matrix, P17-27 shared-backend demo (L3)
- Order applied on 05bf1ab (base of all three patches 3d74df4): L2, L1, L3, each `git apply --3way` (direct-application fallback); all clean, no conflict. Unions checked by hand: `gateway/daemon/process.py` (L1's autostart marker read and removed from the daemon's scope env, `DaemonOptions.idle_exit_seconds`, the idle watch armed at `running`, `terminal_processes` — beside L2's `WsEndpoint` built inside the mount's `try`), `service/remote/host.py` (L2's `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` keeps the journal entry and the presence filter `session.viewers`/`admission.state` — beside L3's `request.settled{answered}` → `server_request.cancelled{answered_elsewhere}`), traps R03.23/R05.1/R05.14/R05.15/R09.11 and ACC-K47-1/K51-1/K51-2. No dependency change, no re-lock (`uv sync --locked` clean); `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile --offline` accepted the lock unchanged.
- **Cross-slice wiring.** Test profiles never idle out mid-test: `tests/fakes/daemon.ProfileHome` (matrix and demo) writes `gateway.idle_exit_seconds: 0` unless a test passes `idle_exit_seconds=`, and the vitest hosts (`apps/tui/src/test/hosts.ts`, whose daemon the grant's `ensure` starts, i.e. armed) do the same; the spool-platform homes never arm (an enabled platform keeps the daemon). New e2e `test_shared_backend.py::test_an_auto_started_daemon_idles_out_and_the_next_chat_restarts_it_silently`: `indus chat -q` with no daemon starts one (detached, armed, 3 s window), it exits by itself with status `stopped`/`idle_exit`/exit 0 and no planned stop, and the next `indus chat -q` starts a new pid and answers with nothing on stderr but the session line (ACC-K47-1). The matrix and the demo run over L2's binding and codes unchanged (attached host sees `OUTCOME_UNKNOWN` on a lost-input retry, profile checks on every dial).
- **Lead decisions applied.** (1) `admission.state`: `AdmissionService(on_state=)` tells every move — admitted `queued` (with its place; skipped when the claim came first), claimed `started` (generation), settled/cancelled/steered/discarded `terminal` (outcome), lost `unknown` (once per pause, beside `on_blocked`); a raising listener never stops the queue; `DaemonSessionHost._admission_moved` appends it to the event log of hosted sessions (strict-mode checked by the WS endpoint tests) (D-16.553). It also moves idle exit's activity fingerprint. (2) `unattended` on `prompt.submit` (`indus -z` sets it through `RemoteSessionService.submit(unattended=)`; the client journal keeps it so an explicit retry stays an exact retry): the ledger payload records `unattended: true` and `_run_turn` switches the session's auto-approve on for that turn only (not when a person's `/yolo` already holds it; follows a compaction's child id; switched off in the runner's `finally`); in-process hosts ignore the field. The matrix agrees on both hosts, so the `cli.z.approvals` allow-list entry is gone (D-16.554). (3) No `generation: 0`: the WS submit waits (≤ 5 s, `CLAIM_WAIT_S`) for the claim of an input that starts at once and answers its turn's generation; still unclaimed → `queued` with the running turn's generation or none; `PromptSubmitResult.generation` is optional (`require_generation()` for the in-process callers in `gateway/core/admission.py` and `gateway/http/turns.py`); the Python attach host and REPL treat a missing one as "not yet known" (D-16.555). (4) Kept: D-16.365, D-16.490. (5) `usage_numbers` has one owner, `service.session_service` (daemon host, `tui_bridge.events`, `methods_session`, `methods_slash`) (D-16.556). Allow-list `D-?` entries numbered (D-16.549 settings methods, D-16.550 session info/title) and the loader now refuses an unnumbered entry.
- **Fix (test, root-caused):** the full targeted run failed once in `test_cli_and_tui_share_one_session_on_one_daemon` ("timed out waiting for the CLI gone"), passing alone. Cause: that run was started under `nohup`, which leaves SIGHUP ignored for every child, so the REPL in the pseudo-terminal never died when the test hung up its terminal. Reproduced deterministically (`nohup pytest -k cli_and_tui` fails at that step); the `Cli` helper now spawns with `preexec_fn` resetting SIGHUP to the default, and the same `nohup` run passes. Product unchanged (a person's closing terminal sends SIGHUP to a REPL that has not ignored it).
- **Security review.** (1) Profile id fail-closed: `check_profile` refuses a missing, non-string or other id; `grant_for` checks `describe` against the client's own `stable_profile_id(home)` before `session-ticket`, the grant's endpoint is checked the same way, `gateway.ready` before any request (close 4005); the product dial always carries the grant's id (`Dialed.profile_id` None only for test dialers). (2) `foreign_listener`: the control client refuses a socket whose peer uid differs from ours before writing (unknown uid allowed: the home is already owner-only). (3) Launch env: `ensure` builds the daemon env through `scrub_launch_env` (switch vars, `INDUS_MODEL`/`PROVIDER`/`CWD`/`LAUNCH_CWD`, every `INDUS_TUI_*`) and sets only `INDUS_GATEWAY_AUTOSTART`, which the daemon removes from its scope env once read; the grant helper uses the same scrub (`test_launch_env_never_reaches_a_daemon_the_grant_starts`). The new `unattended` flag is not env: it is a per-turn auto-approve any owner-minted ticket holder can ask for (the owner can `/yolo` anyway), hard floors still refuse, it is recorded in the ledger payload; caveat: while that turn runs, a steer from another surface into it rides the same switch. (4) Idle exit cannot fire with a non-messaging viewer, a WebSocket connection, an unused ticket, a running turn (fences, admissions, service, intake claims), queued input not behind an unknown turn, a cron run or a job due within the tick, a due loop/heartbeat, a background process, an enabled platform or HTTP part; any probe error counts as busy; loop-only facts are re-checked with nothing awaited before the stop; `admission.state` events now move the fingerprint for any new input. (5) Tickets: only in the subprotocol; the new harnesses use `grant_for` (`Grant.ticket` hidden from `repr`) or `indus gateway grant --json` on a pipe; nothing new logs a ticket; `runtime.describe` carries no ticket. No finding needed a product fix.
- Intended differences recorded: D-16.540 … D-16.557, §2.7 (+ §3.1 row); D-16.558 – D-16.559 unused.
- **P17 "Done when".** Run-until-green commands, the dual-host matrix and the TS-client-against-daemon contract test: green in the gate below. `indus` and `indus --tui` attach to the profile daemon by default and start it when needed; `--local` runs in-process (matrix both hosts; demo step 5): holds. Demo (CLI + TUI half of M6): one daemon, one session; a prompt typed in the CLI streams in both; the approval it raises is answered from the TUI and the CLI card closes; killing the CLI leaves the turn running to completion; restarting the daemon mid-turn shows "outcome unknown" in both and neither resends: holds (`test_cli_and_tui_share_one_session_on_one_daemon`). Not yet: a prompt typed in the TUI shows its reply in the CLI but not the person's text (no event carries it); the dashboard half is P18.
- **Follow-ups.** `session.resume` over the daemon returns no stored messages (an attached TUI reopens on an empty transcript); prompts typed on another surface are not shown (a user-message event or a `turn.started` input preview, protocol); the TUI does not list or `/discard` lost admissions (`admission.state` is there now); D-16.549 (serve the read-only settings/picker methods) and D-16.550 (`session.info` / `session.title` from the daemon); creation policy on the wire (D-16.490) and the attached `-q` deny (D-16.487: carry an unattended-deny the way `-z` now carries approve; closed in wave 47 by `prompt.submit.single_query`, D-16.1051 – D-16.1053); `STALE_GENERATION` from daemon controls (D-16.365); retire `server_request.cancelled` once every renderer reads `request.settled`; a new session has no row until its first turn (D-16.426); Demo 3 SIGKILL-mid-send under both hosts; idle-exit probes for kanban/hosted rooms/handoffs when those land, and the descriptor's capabilities are not emptied while leaving; still open from P17-15..22: `--local` on the durable lease (D-16.488), REPL end on channel loss, resume recap from a daemon snapshot, backups still include the ledger tables (R03.21).
- Gate (verify16, 05bf1ab + L2 + L1 + L3 + integration, `uv sync --locked`, no extras, `HOME`/`INDUS_HOME`/uv cache/pnpm store and cache in the scratchpad): ruff check / format, pyright (0 errors, 0 warnings), lint-imports (1 kept), `python -m indus.protocol.codegen --check` clean; `INDUS_STRESS_SECONDS=10 pytest -m "not perf"` **14591 passed, 79 skipped, 11 deselected** in one run (no failure; L3's two load timeouts — gateway start_status_stop, Feishu media — did not recur); `pytest -m perf` alone 11 passed, 1 skipped; `tooling.checks --require-ref` ok; `CI=true pnpm test --maxWorkers=1` 944 passed (66 files); `pnpm -r typecheck` clean. The demo e2e file (both tests) 3/3 alone after the SIGHUP fix. - **Lead fixes at landing (Agent 2).** (1) `tests/unit/core/subagents/test_batch.py::test_quiet_child_goes_stale_after_450_s` failed once in full run 2 (16 beats, not 15). Root cause: `tests/fakes/turn.drive` moves the fake clock by a whole heartbeat whenever the loop has been idle 0.5 ms of real time; once the 15th quiet check interrupts the child, any real-time gap while the batch winds down (a loaded machine) lets the driver advance 30 s more and the heartbeat beats again before it is cancelled. Reproduced deterministically with a child that takes 5 ms of real time to return after its interrupt (20 beats). The product decision is right (stale at 450 s); the test counted beats after it. The test now pins the moment of the interrupt (`FakeRun.interrupted_at` = 450 s) and the 15th beat's time; product unchanged. (2) `pnpm lint` / `pnpm fmt:check` were red on main from 22 Python-test data files (JSON under `tests/fixtures/**` and `tests/fakes/backend/sdk_surfaces.json`, some byte-exact vectors): `biome.json` now excludes `tests/fixtures` and `tests/fakes/**/*.json` (TypeScript lives under `apps/` and `packages/`); both commands exit 0.
- Gate after the lead fixes: `test_batch.py` 12 passed; ruff / pyright clean on it; `pnpm lint`, `pnpm fmt:check` exit 0. The two full non-perf runs above (run 2: only the test_batch failure, now fixed) stand for the rest.

The ACP daemon no longer fakes `describe`: the real endpoint answers `runtime.describe`; a test changes what is advertised through `AcpDaemon.capabilities` (read by the host instance, so the next `runtime.describe` and the control socket see it) and the claimed profile through `profile_id` (a dial refuses another profile before any ticket, as `grant_for` does). A started input is answered `cancelled: false, state: started, generation` and never stopped by admission id; the attached host then interrupts that generation (D-16.607).
  - `config.set show_reasoning`: not added — the daemon streams `reasoning.delta` to every viewer, so the attached host's `show_reasoning` is a no-op like the local host's and nothing is sent on reconnect (D-16.603). (`show_reasoning` is not even a `ConfigKey`.)

- **Fix (test, root-caused):** the first full run failed once in `photon/test_adapter.py::test_a_dropped_stream_is_reopened_after_1_s_doubling` (`waits[0]` was "photon: connected to the sidecar on port 8789"), passing alone. Cause: the test read every captured log record and assumed INFO is filtered out; `foundation.log`'s setup lowers the root logger to the file level (INFO) and earlier tests in the same process that run it leave it there, so the adapter's INFO connect line is captured too. Reproduced deterministically alone with `-o log_level=INFO` (fails at that assertion); the test now reads only WARNING-and-above records (the reopen lines) and passes alone, under `-o log_level=INFO` and `DEBUG`; product unchanged. Every test directory the patches touch then ran green under `-o log_level=DEBUG` (947 passed, 54 skipped), so no other test leans on the default level.
- **Fix (test, root-caused, pre-existing on main — the "gateway start_status_stop" load timeout L3 saw):** the second full run failed in `tests/unit/cli/commands/test_gateway.py::test_start_status_stop_without_a_service` (`status` said "starting (pid …, answered on the control socket)"); alone it passed 8 of 9 and failed once. Cause: `indus gateway start` returns when the control socket answers, which is the daemon's first start stage; the state turns `running` only after the platforms connect, recovery runs, the session host opens and the background parts and WS endpoint are up (`GatewayDaemon._start` → `_running`), and the test read `status` once, at once. Reproduced deterministically with a copy of the spool plugin whose `_open` waits 1.5 s (fails every time at that assertion). The test now asks `status` until it no longer says `starting` (bounded 20 s, like `--wait 20`) and then checks the same text; it passes with the slow plugin (the whole file, 13) and alone 3× (13 each). Product unchanged (`start` documents that it waits for the control socket to answer).
- **Third full run: `tests/unit/learning/memory/test_providers.py::test_slow_prefetch_bounded_by_eight_seconds`** ("no clock sleeper appeared"; alone 3 of 3 failures while the machine was loaded). Same root cause the lead fixed on main in ca2860b (the helper waited 500 loop turns for a deadline armed after a worker-thread hop); reproduced here by delaying `on_turn_start` 0.2 s on its thread. The file now carries ca2860b's content exactly (applied as a diff, not committed; identical to `ca2860b:tests/unit/learning/memory/test_providers.py`, so the rebase is a no-op there): 21 passed with the delayed thread and alone.
- No product fix was needed beyond the WeCom `push` change the README asked for. Two first-draft assertions of my own tests were corrected by reading the behaviour: Signal replies to the sender's account id (its UUID, `signal_wire.uuid_for`), not the number; a whole-turn stream sends one blank-line-only frame after a tool call (harmless, still prefix-stable).
- **Security review (combined diff).** Photon: no credential row, so env alone never enables it; the wizard stores both credentials in the secret store only (never `.env`/config, never printed — the parametrised wizard test checks the disk and output); the provider module is operator configuration, as for WhatsApp; sidecar loopback-only, per-start Bearer token compared in constant time, `Host` check, 64 KiB bodies, a port held by another process never adopted or signalled, child env minimal, token never in argv, numbers masked in logs (E15's tests); the target rule refuses anything outside the four forms (no separators, no `;` variants beyond `any;-;`/`any;+;`). Doctor: the Photon rows touch no network (PATH lookup, file existence, secret presence); probes stay bounded to configured addresses. Native streams: frames are the redactor's output with media directives removed; cut at the last whitespace so a token-shaped secret is whole before it is masked; a frame the mask rewrites is skipped (never sent) and the final carries the masked text; an approval seals the stream so the card is never inside a streamed message; drafts only in private chats, Slack streams only in a thread. Discord voice: `/voice` gated like any message (allow-all, allowlist, role, pairing), speech only into the joined channel, leave before close. Stickers: described before the core's authorization but bounded (private chats / addressed group messages, 10 fresh per sender per hour, cached) — the follow-up seam moves it after authorization. No credential-shaped literal in the diff (fake tokens built at run time; the only `sk-` hits are the word "task-card"); no `hermes`/`nous` outside `docs/`; nothing written under `/root` or `~` (HOME, INDUS_HOME, uv cache, npm cache and pnpm store in the scratchpad; `find /root -newer` the run start is empty outside the harness's own directories).

- **Download tickets (lead decision 3, security, D-16.596).** New `web/auth/download_ticket.py` (`DownloadTickets`: single use — any presentation of a known ticket spends it; 30 s TTL, constructor refuses > 60; bound to the resolved path; per dashboard process; SHA-256 digests only; ≤ 256 live, spent ones bounded too). `POST /api/files/download-ticket {path}` (same root / secret-name rules as a read; `no-store`) → `{ticket, expires_in}`; `GET /api/files/download?path=&ticket=` redeems the ticket **before** looking at the path (an unauthenticated caller learns nothing about the disk: five probes, one identical 401 body), then re-resolves the path and compares (a symlink swapped in after minting is re-checked). Gate: `QUERY_TOKEN_PATHS` → `TICKET_PATHS` (loopback hands the route to its handler; gated mode still wants the cookie as well), and any `/api` request naming `token` in its query is 401 `credential_in_url`, beside a good header too. SPA: `fileDownloadUrl` mints, `downloadFile` clicks a credential-free link, Files page buttons instead of `<a href>` (toasts a refusal); tests updated (`FilesPage.test.tsx` ×2) and the mock API mints. Tests: good once; other path refused and spends it; expired (fake clock); another profile's dashboard refuses it (and it stays good where minted); no-ticket probes identical; gated cookie + ticket; tickets never in a server log record or any file under the home (the audit log included) — the test client's own httpx request log is the browser's side and is excluded.

The dashboard's stop/restart actions now work end to end: `tests/integration/web/test_daemon_stop.py` (real in-process daemon in tmp_path + the real dashboard app): stop → daemon exits 0, status `planned_stop:dashboard`, record consumed, `planned_stop_reason` says the next attach leaves it stopped; restart → `planned_stop:restart`, the ensure seam is called once with `planned_stop_reason(home) is None` (so the real ensure would start it); a second stop changes nothing; the label is sanitised. R3's "older daemon" message path kept (fake control without `stop`).
